My hygiene is okay, but I feel completely blank inside and can't motivate myself to apply for jobs or go to church. I just don't have the drive to do anything else.\n\nDoctor: When you have these thoughts about people watching you or talking in code, do you feel like these ideas are based on actual events you've witnessed, or do you feel like they are a fixed belief that you are certain is true despite what others might say?\n\nPatient: I feel like it's based on actual things I see happening, like when the ladies at church or the nurses at the clinic suddenly stop talking and glance over at me. I know it probably sounds strange to other people, but seeing them do that makes me certain they are keeping an eye on me and communicating in code.\n\nDoctor: Besides the suspicion that people are watching you, have you ever experienced any other unusual beliefs, such as feeling that you have a special mission, that you are being controlled by an outside force, or that you have special powers?\n\nPatient: No, I've never experienced anything like that. I don't feel like I have any special powers or missions, and I don't feel controlled by anyone else.\n\nDoctor: Has there ever been a period of at least a month where you experienced these suspicious thoughts about people watching you, but without the persistent 'blank' feeling and loss of drive that you've been describing?"

No, there has never been a time like that. The shut down and blank feelings have been constant for over two years now, while the suspicious thoughts only started a couple of months ago. So those thoughts have only ever happened while I was already feeling this way.

Could you please tell me what brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I feel like I’ve lost the drive to do anything or be around people. It's just getting harder and harder to talk or care about things the way I used to.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way, and have you experienced similar episodes like this in the past?\n\nPatient: It started right after I graduated high school, so it's been going on for a little over two years now. It hasn't really been separate episodes, just this slow, continuous shutting down that has kept getting worse.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you have special, unusual ways of thinking, or do you sometimes feel like people are talking about you or that you have a unique connection to events around you?\n\nPatient: Yes, actually, over the last couple of months I've noticed that the ladies at my church and the nurses at the clinic seem to be checking up on me. When they pause their conversations or glance over at me, I can tell they're talking in code because they've been told to keep an eye on me. It's a really strange feeling, but I'm pretty sure it's happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced periods where you heard voices that others couldn't hear, or have these concerns about people talking about you been your primary focus?\n\nPatient: No, I've never heard any voices or sounds that other people couldn't hear. My main concern has just been that feeling that the people at church and the clinic are watching me and talking in code, which I only started noticing in the last couple of months.\n\nDoctor: Besides these thoughts about being watched, have you noticed any other changes, such as your speech becoming disorganized or your behavior becoming noticeably erratic or unusual to others?\n\nPatient: No, my speech doesn't feel disorganized, but I feel blank inside and my words just come out very short and flat now. My family really noticed when I completely stopped going to church and withdrew from my usual activities.\n\nDoctor: Besides the feeling of being watched, have you noticed any other changes, such as a significant decline in your ability to take care of your personal hygiene or a loss of interest in almost all daily activities?\n\nPatient: Yes, I've lost interest in almost everything and spend most of my days just sitting in my room or by the garden staring. So those thoughts have only ever happened while I was already feeling this way.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced any periods of disorganized speech, or have you noticed your thoughts becoming so muddled that others have difficulty following what you are saying?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really experienced muddled thoughts or disorganized speech where others can't follow what I'm saying. It's more that I just feel blank inside, and my words tend to come out very short and flat.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced any period where your behavior was clearly disorganized, such as wearing unusual clothing, acting in a way that seemed bizarre to others, or struggling with basic tasks like cooking or cleaning?\n\nPatient: No, I've never really experienced anything like that. Reply naturally in English as the Patient to the last Doctor utterance.\nAnswer only the question, in 2–3 sentences maximum.\n\n[Dialogue]\nDoctor: Hello, I am Dr. Smith. Could you please tell me what brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I feel like I’ve lost the drive to do anything or be around people. It's just getting harder and harder to talk or care about things the way I used to.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way, and have you experienced similar episodes like this in the past?\n\nPatient: It started right after I graduated high school, so it's been going on for a little over two years now. It hasn't really been separate episodes, just this slow, continuous shutting down that has kept getting worse.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you have special, unusual ways of thinking, or do you sometimes feel like people are talking about you or that you have a unique connection to events around you?\n\nPatient: Yes, actually, over the last couple of months I've noticed that the ladies at my church and the nurses at the clinic seem to be checking up on me. When they pause their conversations or glance over at me, I can tell they're talking in code because they've been told to keep an eye on me. Subtypes: Persecutory Delusions, Referential Delusions, Grandiose Delusions, Erotomanic Delusions, Nihilistic Delusions, Somatic Delusions, Jealous Delusions\n[S020] Hallucinations: Perception-like experiences that occur without an external stimulus and are as vivid and clear as normal perception.  Subtypes: Auditory Hallucinations, Visual Hallucinations, Tactile Hallucinations, Olfactory Hallucinations, Gustatory Hallucinations\n[S021] Disorganized_Thinking_Speech: Symptoms where speech or thought appears scattered and difficult to follow due to a lack of logical or consistent flow.  Subtypes: Derailment or Loose Associations, Tangentiality, Incoherence or Word Salad, Neologisms, Clang Associations\n[S022] Disorganized_Abnormal_Behavior: A state where behavior and motor control are severely disturbed, appearing as movements or reactions inappropriate to the situation.  Subtypes: Childlike Silliness, Stupor, Catalepsy, Waxy Flexibility, Mutism, Negativism, Posturing, Mannerism, Stereotypy, Agitation, Grimacing, Echolalia, Echopraxia\n[S023] Negative_Symptoms: A state where emotions, motivation, speech, or social interest that should normally be present are diminished or lost.  Subtypes: Diminished Emotional Expression, Avolition, Alogia, Anhedonia, Asociality\n[S024] Depressed_Mood: A state where a sad, empty, or hopeless mood persists.  Subtypes: Depressed Mood, Tearfulness, Sadness, Emptiness, Emotional Numbness\n[S025] Loss_of_Interest: A state where interest or pleasure in previously enjoyed activities is markedly diminished.  Subtypes: Loss of Interest, Social Withdrawal, Loss of Motivation, Anhedonia, Indifference to Achievement\n[S026] Weight_Appetite_Change: Significant increase or decrease in appetite or weight.  Subtypes: Significant Weight Loss, Weight Gain, Decrease in Appetite, Increase in Appetite\n[S027] Sleep_Disturbance: A state where there is a significant change in the quantity or quality of sleep.  Subtypes: Initial Insomnia, Middle Insomnia, Early Morning Awakening, Hypersomnia, Nightmare Disorder, Non-Restorative Sleep\n[S028] Psychomotor_Changes: Observable increase or decrease in psychomotor activity.  Subtypes: Psychomotor Agitation, Psychomotor Retardation (Motor), Psychomotor Retardation (Speech)\n[S029] Fatigue: Persistent fatigue that is not relieved by rest and a marked lack of energy.  Subtypes: Chronic Fatigue, Physical Fatigue, Mental Fatigue, Loss of Energy\n[S030] Worthlessness_Guilt: Feelings of worthlessness or excessive or inappropriate guilt (which may be delusional; not merely self-reproach or guilt about being sick). Subtypes: Recurrent Thoughts of Death, Suicidal Ideation, Suicidal Impulse, Specific Plan for Suicide, Suicide Attempt\n[S083] Hopelessness: A feeling of despair or having no hope for the future.  Subtypes: Future Hopelessness, Belief in Impossibility of Improvement, Helpless Resignation, Chronic Pessimism\n[S033] Elevated_Irritable_Mood: A distinct period of abnormally and persistently elevated, expansive, or irritable mood.  Subtypes: Elevated Mood, Irritable Mood, Mood Lability\n[S034] Increased_Activity: Abnormally and persistently increased goal-directed activity or energy.  Subtypes: Increased Energy, Hyperactivity, Psychomotor Agitation\n[S035] Inflated_Self_Esteem: Inflated self-esteem or grandiosity (may range from uncritical self-confidence to delusional proportions). Subtypes: Occupational Hyperactivity, Social Hyperactivity, Hypersexuality, Psychomotor Agitation\n[S041] Painful_Consequence_Behavior: Excessive involvement in activities that have a high potential for painful consequences. Subtypes: Fear of Weight Gain, Fear of Specific Foods, Obsession with Weight Gain\n[S046] Body_Image_Weight_Shape_Disturbance: Undue influence of body weight or shape on self-evaluation. Subtypes: Self-Induced Vomiting, Misuse of Medications\n[S049] Eating_Large_Amount: Eating, in a discrete period of time, an amount of food that is definitely larger than what most individuals would eat in a similar period of time under similar circumstances.\n[S050] Loss_of_Control: A sense of lack of control over eating during the episode (e.g., a feeling that one cannot stop eating or control what or how much one is eating).\n[S051] Rapid_Eating: Eating much more rapidly than normal.\n[S052] Eating_Until_Uncomfortably_Full: Eating until feeling uncomfortably full.\n[S053] Eating_Without_Hunger: Eating large amounts of food when not feeling physically hungry.\n[S054] Eating_Alone_Due_to_Embarrassment: Eating alone because of feeling embarrassed by how much one is eating.\n[S055] Negative_Affect_After_Binge: Feeling disgusted with oneself, depressed, or very guilty afterward.\n[S084] Low_Weight_Severity_Non_Recognition: Persistent lack of recognition of the seriousness of the current low body weight. Subtypes: Cleaning or Washing Compulsions, Checking Compulsions, Ordering and Arranging Compulsions, Mental Acts, Repeating Rituals\n[S067] Preoccupation_with_Appearance: Preoccupation with one or more perceived defects or flaws in physical appearance that are not observable or appear slight to others. Subtypes: Intrusive Memories, Distressing Dreams, Dissociative Reactions (Flashbacks), Psychological Distress at Cues, Physiological Reactions to Cues\n[S070] Trauma_Avoidance: Persistent avoidance of stimuli associated with the traumatic event.  Subtypes: Internal Avoidance, External Avoidance\n[S071] Trauma_Related_Amnesia: Inability to remember an important aspect of the traumatic event (typically due to dissociative amnesia and not to other factors like head injury or alcohol).  Subtypes: Fragmented Memory, Generalized Amnesia\n[S072] Negative_Beliefs: Persistent and exaggerated negative beliefs or expectations about oneself, others, or the world. Subtypes: Self-Blame, Blaming Others\n[S074] Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State: Persistent negative emotional state (e.g., fear, horror, anger, guilt, or shame).  Subtypes: Persistent Fear, Persistent Horror, Persistent Anger, Persistent Guilt, Persistent Shame\n[S075] Social_Detachment: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others.  Subtypes: Emotional Detachment, Social Isolation, Difficulty with Intimacy, Loss of Belonging\n[S076] Inability_Positive_Emotions: Persistent inability to experience positive emotions (e.g., inability to experience happiness, satisfaction, or loving feelings).  Subtypes: Anhedonia for Happiness, Lack of Satisfaction, Inability to Feel Love, Emotional Numbness\n[S077] Irritability_Aggression: Irritable behavior and angry outbursts (with little or no provocation) typically expressed as verbal or physical aggression.  Subtypes: Impulsive Anger, Verbal Aggression, Physical Aggression, Chronic Irritability\n[S078] Reckless_Self_Destructive_Behavior: Reckless or self-destructive behavior pattern that endangers one's safety or health. Subtypes: With Depressed Mood, With Anxiety, With Mixed Anxiety and Depressed Mood, With Disturbance of Conduct, With Mixed Disturbance of Emotions and Conduct, Unspecified\n[S082] Depersonalization_Derealization: Altered sense of the reality of one's self or surroundings after a traumatic event.
